Buckets : (1) `setUserMetadataValue` create + upsert + arbitrary JSON-shaped values (object / array / number) so the `Json` column round-trips correctly. (2) `getUserMetadataValue` returns `undefined` for missing tuples, the stored value for real rows, and crucially does NOT leak across users for the same `(module, key)`. (3) `listUserMetadataForModule` returns rows for one user × one module sorted by key, excludes other-module rows, returns empty for users with no metadata. (4) `deleteUserMetadataValue` removes by tuple + is idempotent on missing rows (no throw). (5) `deleteUserMetadataForModule` returns `{ count }`, doesn't touch other modules' rows or other users' rows — both the cross-module and cross-user isolation guarantees are explicit specs because the `deleteMany` `WHERE` shape is the kind of code that can pass typecheck and silently drop the wrong rows. (6) Validation — invalid module names, empty / oversized / pattern-violating keys all reject with `ValidationError`, with separate cases pinning that the read paths (`listUserMetadataForModule`, `getUserMetadataValue`) validate too (callers expect rejection at the boundary, not silently-empty results).
  - **Net effect.** `src/server/read.ts` lifts from 0 % → 100 % covered ; `src/server/metadata.ts` lifts from 0 % → near-100 %. Package floor bumps from `25/25/55/75` to `75/75/85/85`. The `event-types.ts` + `permissions.ts` registration shims are still at 0 % (called once at api boot, not exercised by any spec) ; covering them would push the floor higher but adds little signal because they're table-driven `register*()` calls.

- 2026-05-08: Feature-flag write + sync integration suite. Picks up the lowest-coverage package on the merged baseline (37 % lines → 86 % lines) and lifts every previously-untested file in `src/server/`. Floor bumped from 30 → 80 in [tools/merge-coverage.ts](monark/app/tools/merge-coverage.ts).
  - **`@monark/feature-flags` write + sync** ([packages/feature-flags/tests/integration/write-and-sync.test.ts](monark/app/packages/feature-flags/tests/integration/write-and-sync.test.ts)). 17 cases against the Postgres testcontainer covering the write path that the existing `is-enabled.test.ts` (read path) didn't reach. Buckets : (1) `syncFlagsToDatabase` registry → DB fan-out — every registered flag lands in `FeatureFlag`, idempotent on re-run, registry-side description / defaultOn changes flow through, dropping a flag from the registry leaves the orphan row (sync is additive, never destructive — operator removes orphans manually). (2) `setOverride` happy path + idempotency — new row at global / org / user / role scope, re-targeting the same scope updates instead of duplicating, the `feature-flag.flipped` event carries the full payload (module, flagKey, scope, enabled, actorId, occurredAt). (3) `setOverride` validation — unknown flag key → ValidationError, multi-target scope (org + user) → ValidationError, missing actorId → ValidationError. (4) `removeOverride` — deletes the row by id, missing actorId rejected. (5) `writeOverride` scope-distinct row creation — same flag at different orgs / different users / a role-scoped slot all land as separate rows so the resolver's precedence logic has the right inputs. (6) `deleteOverride` raw delete by id. (7) `listFlagDefinitions` projection shape (registry → dotted-key + module + description + defaultOn). The `ROLE_A` test fixture uses `findFirst + create` for the same Prisma compound-unique-with-null-orgId workaround the rbac integration tests landed earlier today.
  - **Net effect.** `src/server/write.ts` and `src/server/sync.ts` go from 0 % → 100 % covered ; `src/server/data.ts` lifts substantially. The package-level floor moves from `lines/stmts/funcs/branches: 30/30/30/75` to `80/80/75/85`. `permissions.ts` and `event-types.ts` are still at 0 % (they're trivial registration shims that run at module-load in real boot but aren't exercised by any spec) ; they'd push the floor to ~95 % if covered, but that's a bigger rearrangement and not blocking the threshold gate as it stands.

- 2026-05-08: Integration-suite rescue + post-merge threshold gate. The first merged-coverage run on local (after the merge infra landed earlier today) surfaced that 6 of 8 integration suites had pre-existing test bugs that turbo's "bail on first failure" had been hiding ; only one package's failure ever surfaced in CI logs while the rest were silently cancelled. This batch fixes every one and lands a CI gate that breaks the build if any package drops below its measured baseline.
  - **Pre-existing test bugs found + fixed.** Six categories : (1) `db.role.upsert({ where: { key_organizationId: { key, organizationId: null } } })` — Prisma rejects null in compound-unique `where` clauses even when the underlying `@@unique([key, organizationId])` constraint allows null-distinct rows in PG ; replaced with `findFirst({ where: { key, organizationId: null } })` + create across [packages/rbac/tests/integration/write.test.ts](monark/app/packages/rbac/tests/integration/write.test.ts), [read.test.ts](monark/app/packages/rbac/tests/integration/read.test.ts), [role-crud.test.ts](monark/app/packages/rbac/tests/integration/role-crud.test.ts), [packages/organizations/tests/integration/invites.test.ts](monark/app/packages/organizations/tests/integration/invites.test.ts), [packages/notifications/tests/integration/subscribers.test.ts](monark/app/packages/notifications/tests/integration/subscribers.test.ts). (2) `seedUser` in [packages/users/tests/integration/data.test.ts](monark/app/packages/users/tests/integration/data.test.ts) used `??` on `emailVerifiedAt` / `disabledAt` / `deletedAt`, which converts an explicit `null` override back to the default value (since null is nullish) ; switched to `'key' in overrides` so explicit-null seeds round-trip. (3) `subscribers.test.ts` truncated `Role` in `afterEach` but seeded built-in roles in `beforeAll`, so every spec after the first failed to find SYSADMIN/ADMIN ; moved seeding to `beforeEach`. (4) `dispatch.test.ts` never called `registerCoreNotificationKinds()` so `notify("auth.password-changed", …)` short-circuited with empty deliveryIds ; added the registration call. (5) `read.test.ts` imported `assignRole` from `data.ts` instead of `write.ts` ; corrected the import. (6) `RoleAssignment_roleId_fkey` is `ON DELETE RESTRICT` in the migration despite the schema declaring `Cascade` (drift) ; reordered every rbac test's `afterEach` to clear `RoleAssignment` before `Role` to satisfy the constraint. Several smaller fixes too : `role-crud.countActiveAssignments` test referenced fake user IDs via `createMany` ; `write.test.ts` referenced fake `grantedById` / `revokedById` actor IDs ; both now seed the corresponding User rows. The `webhooks/rotateEndpointSecret` function returned `Promise<void>` but the integration test expected the updated row ; promoted the return type to `Promise<EndpointWithSubs>` (the router caller already discarded the value, so no other callers affected). The `api/server` cron-auth happy-path expected `body.processed` but `processExpiredDeletions` returns `{ attempted, succeeded, failed }` ; updated the test assertion to match the actual shape.
  - **Post-merge threshold gate** ([tools/merge-coverage.ts](monark/app/tools/merge-coverage.ts)). Per-package floors live in a `THRESHOLDS` map at the top of the merge script ; after the merge produces each package's `coverage-final.json`, the script reads the merged percentages and exits non-zero if any metric dips below its floor. Floors are set 5pts below the measured baseline (rounded to nearest 5%) so a regression breaks the build but the floor doesn't sit on the current number. Per-metric instead of single-floor because measured branches and lines diverge meaningfully (e.g. `services/api` is 73% lines but 53% branches, `services/web` is 8% lines but 89% branches because the few covered files are well-tested). Vitest's per-run `coverage.thresholds` config stays commented out — the merged number is what matters, and per-run thresholds would either fail spuriously (unit-only run for a package whose data layer is integration-tested) or duplicate the merge-script gate.
  - **Measured baselines** (first clean merged run) :
    | package | lines | stmts | funcs | branches |
    |---|---|---|---|---|
    | auth | 67.16% | 67.16% | 80.00% | 89.05% |
    | common | 62.73% | 62.73% | 100.00% | 88.46% |
    | feature-flags | 36.89% | 36.89% | 35.71% | 82.00% |
    | notifications | 67.20% | 67.20% | 91.30% | 87.50% |
    | organizations | 58.57% | 58.57% | 75.00% | 85.00% |
    | rbac | 58.73% | 58.73% | 60.00% | 88.78% |
    | users | 34.23% | 34.23% | 60.00% | 80.00% |
    | webhooks | 49.39% | 49.39% | 63.88% | 87.61% |
    | services/api | 73.01% | 73.01% | 78.94% | 53.03% |
    | services/web | 8.00% | 8.00% | 68.98% | 89.09% |
  - **Net effect.** Every integration suite now passes locally + in CI (turbo's `--continue` flag surfaces every package's results so a failure in one doesn't mask another's success or failure). The threshold gate locks in the measured baseline ; any future change that drops a metric below its floor breaks `pnpm coverage:merge` and the corresponding CI step, so coverage erosion can't slip past review. Floors will ratchet up as we add suites against the surfaces still under-covered (the test plan calls out `services/web` action handlers + `users` data layer + `feature-flags` write/sync paths as the next batch).

- 2026-05-08: Coverage merge infrastructure. Unblocks the per-package `coverage.thresholds` gates that have been deferred since the test push started ; before this, the unit and integration runs each overwrote `coverage/lcov.info` so Codecov saw whichever ran last and the data-layer files (which only run under integration) showed near-0%. Per-package thresholds will get flipped on in a follow-up commit once the first merged CI run gives real baselines to set the floors against.
  - **Per-run subdirectories.** Every unit `vitest.config.ts` now sets `coverage.reportsDirectory: "coverage/unit"` ; every `vitest.integration.config.ts` sets `coverage.reportsDirectory: "coverage/integration"` and an explicit `include: ["src/**/*.ts"]` so the integration run reports against the same file set as the unit run (without it, coverage-v8 would only count files actually loaded during the integration suite, which artificially deflates the number). 9 unit configs + 7 integration configs touched ; `services/web` still uses `coverage/unit/` even though it has no integration counterpart, for merge-script discovery consistency.
  - **`json` reporter added to the shared base** ([vitest.shared.ts](monark/app/vitest.shared.ts)). Lands `coverage-final.json` per run — that's what the merge script reads ; `lcov` / `text` / `html` keep doing what they did. Centralised so neither the unit nor the integration override has to think about it.
  - **`test:integration:coverage` script per package.** Mirrors `test:coverage` for the integration config — `vitest run --config vitest.integration.config.ts --coverage --passWithNoTests`. Added to all 8 packages with an integration suite (auth / users / organizations / rbac / notifications / webhooks / feature-flags / services/api). Root-level `pnpm test:integration:coverage` runs `turbo run test:integration:coverage` so packages without the script `--passWithNoTests` cleanly. New `test:integration:coverage` task in [turbo.json](monark/app/turbo.json) declares `coverage/**` as the output the cache should track.
  - **`tools/merge-coverage.ts`** ([tools/merge-coverage.ts](monark/app/tools/merge-coverage.ts)). Walks `packages/*/coverage/{unit,integration}/coverage-final.json` + `services/*/coverage/{unit,integration}/coverage-final.json`, fuses each pair via `istanbul-lib-coverage`'s `createCoverageMap()`+`merge()`, writes the merged JSON to `coverage/coverage-final.json` and regenerates `coverage/lcov.info` via `istanbul-reports`'s `lcov` reporter (with `projectRoot: pkg.absPath` so the lcov SF lines stay relative — Codecov needs that). Skips packages with neither run cleanly. Prints a per-package summary table : lines / statements / functions / branches percentages alongside which sources contributed (unit, integration, or unit+integration), so a CI log scan tells you at a glance which packages are running which suites and what the merged number looks like. New root devDeps : `istanbul-lib-coverage`, `istanbul-lib-report`, `istanbul-reports` (already transitive via `@vitest/coverage-v8`, promoted to direct so `tsx` can resolve them) + the matching `@types/istanbul-*` packages. New `coverage:merge` root script.
  - **CI wiring** ([.github/workflows/ci.yml](monark/app/.github/workflows/ci.yml)). The `verify` job's "Test (integration)" step is now "Test (integration, with coverage)" running `pnpm test:integration:coverage` ; a new "Merge coverage (unit + integration)" step runs `pnpm coverage:merge` before the Codecov upload. The Codecov upload step still points at each package's `coverage/lcov.info` — the merge script overwrites that file with the merged version, so no upload-side change was needed.
  - **Net effect.** Codecov will start reflecting real merged numbers on the next CI run instead of unit-only numbers (which were near-0% for any file whose tests live under `tests/integration/`). The per-package threshold gates can now flip on against measured baselines. The per-run JSON files stay on disk under `coverage/{unit,integration}/` for diffing or re-merging without rerunning the suite.

- 2026-05-08: `services/api` integration suite + server.ts entrypoint refactor. Closes the last "no integration coverage at all" gap on the test plan and pins the api's HTTP surface contract.
  - **Entrypoint guard refactor** ([services/api/src/server.ts](monark/app/services/api/src/server.ts)). The express app stays built at module-load (every `register*()` call is idempotent + cheap), but `app.listen()` + the webhook delivery worker's `setInterval` + `void syncFlagsToDatabase()` + `void maybeBootstrapSingletonOrg()` now run only when this file is the process entrypoint. The check compares `process.argv[1]` against `fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)`, which holds under both `tsx` (dev / start) and `node dist/server.js` (built). `pnpm dev` and `pnpm start` keep working unchanged ; importing the module from a test gets a fully-built but inert app suitable for supertest. The `app` const is now exported alongside `maybeBootstrapSingletonOrg` so tests can drive both directly.
  - **Integration suite** ([services/api/tests/integration/server.test.ts](monark/app/services/api/tests/integration/server.test.ts) + [services/api/vitest.integration.config.ts](monark/app/services/api/vitest.integration.config.ts)). 13 cases covering : (1) the boot path itself — importing `app` doesn't throw, every module-level `register*()` lands successfully (a regression in any of those surfaces as an import-time error here, before the test body) ; (2) `/health` returns the documented `{ status: "ok", service: "api" }` shape so platform health checks stay green ; (3) cron auth for `/cron/process-account-deletions` + `/cron/sweep-webhook-deliveries` — missing header → 401, wrong bearer → 401, bare token without "Bearer " prefix → 401, right bearer → 200 with the helper result handed through unchanged ; (4) CORS preflight from the default `WEB_ORIGIN` resolves with credentials + the echoed origin ; (5) the `/trpc` mount stays attached (unknown-procedure 4xx returns the expected JSON error shape — pins that the express adapter is reachable so a future refactor can't accidentally drop the entire tRPC surface) ; (6) `maybeBootstrapSingletonOrg` is callable + idempotent. New `vitest.integration.config.ts` + `test:integration` script + `@monark/test-utils` workspace devDep + `supertest` / `@types/supertest` devDeps.
  - **Net effect.** The api boot-path regression net is closed : a missing register call, an env validation gap, a route mount ordering bug, or a CORS rule typo all break this suite before they reach staging. Combined with the prior batches, every package + the api service now has integration coverage on every push.

- 2026-05-08: Two more integration suites (round 3). Notification subscribers (the cross-module wiring layer) and feature-flag scoped resolution (the runtime-decision layer) now have direct CI gates instead of being implicitly tested by surrounding suites.
  - **`@monark/notifications` subscribers** ([packages/notifications/tests/integration/subscribers.test.ts](monark/app/packages/notifications/tests/integration/subscribers.test.ts)). 14 cases covering every domain event the subscribers bind to. Mocks `notify` at the module-import boundary via `vi.mock("../../src/server/dispatch", …)` so the spec focuses on the wiring contract — each event maps to the right notification kind, the right recipient(s), and the right data shape — without re-testing the dispatch path (which has its own integration suite). Covers : the auth events (`trusted-device.added` → `auth.new-device` with the device row joined in, plus the silent fall-through when the row's gone ; `user.password-changed`, `totp.enabled`, `totp.disabled`, `trusted-devices.all-revoked`) ; the account-lifecycle events (`user.email-changed`, `user.deletion-requested`, `user.deletion-canceled`) ; the webhook operator alerts (org-scoped permanent failures fan out to org admins with `scope: "org"`, platform-tier permanent failures fan out to sysadmins with `scope: "platform"`, transient failures stay silent because `permanent === false`, the `endpoint-disabled-after-failures` event looks up the URL and falls back to a sentinel when the row's already gone). Plus the registration-idempotency guard : calling `registerNotificationSubscribers()` twice doesn't double-bind handlers (would cause every event to fire each subscriber twice). New `_resetSubscribersForTesting` helper exported from `@monark/notifications/server` so the `beforeEach` can re-bind from scratch.
  - **`@monark/feature-flags` is-enabled / getFlags integration** ([packages/feature-flags/tests/integration/is-enabled.test.ts](monark/app/packages/feature-flags/tests/integration/is-enabled.test.ts) + [packages/feature-flags/vitest.integration.config.ts](monark/app/packages/feature-flags/vitest.integration.config.ts)). 12 cases against a Postgres testcontainer covering the scoped-precedence resolution end-to-end (the `mostSpecific` pure function had unit coverage already ; this locks in that the SQL `WHERE` shape returns exactly the rows the resolver expects). Three buckets : (1) registry default fallback (off-by-default + on-by-default + unknown-flag fallthrough), (2) global override beats default in both directions, (3) scoped precedence — user-scoped beats org/role/global, org-scoped beats global with no user override, user-scoped doesn't leak across users, org-scoped doesn't leak across orgs. Also covers `getFlags` multi-key batching, the empty-key short-circuit, and the `(module, flagKey) → FeatureFlag` FK cascade so removing a flag definition cleans up its overrides automatically. New `vitest.integration.config.ts` + `@monark/test-utils` workspace devDep + `test:integration` script ; the package now participates in the CI verify job's `test:integration` step.
  - **Net effect.** Notifications subscribers are the seam between every module's domain events and the user-facing inbox + email pipeline ; a regression there would silently swallow a security alert. Feature-flag scoped resolution is the seam every runtime decision (enable / disable / variant pick) routes through ; a regression there could silently flip a critical-path flag for the wrong scope. Both surfaces now break the build before they reach staging.

- 2026-05-08: Two more integration suites against the test-plan list. Both target surfaces flagged as "load-bearing, no current coverage" : org-invite token hashing + atomic accept (security-critical) and rbac write-side scope guards + event-emission contract (authorization-critical).
  - **`@monark/organizations` invites** ([packages/organizations/tests/integration/invites.test.ts](monark/app/packages/organizations/tests/integration/invites.test.ts)). 14 cases against the Postgres testcontainer. Pins three load-bearing guarantees : (1) the plaintext invite token is **never** persisted — only its SHA-256 hash lands on `Invite.tokenHash` (verified by serializing the row and asserting the plaintext doesn't appear anywhere) ; (2) acceptance is atomic — invite stamping + OrganizationMembership upsert + RoleAssignment creation all in one transaction, with idempotent re-accept on an already-accepted invite returning the same shape (no double membership rows) ; (3) `consumePendingInvitesForUser` applies every matching invite for the user's email. Also covers email normalization (lowercase + trim), invalid-email + unknown-org rejection, expired-invite rejection, the displayName "pre-fill only when null, never overwrite" rule, and revoked invites becoming unaccepted. `sendMail` short-circuits because the test env leaves `SMTP_URL` unset (same pattern as the notifications dispatch integration).
  - **`@monark/rbac` write-side** ([packages/rbac/tests/integration/write.test.ts](monark/app/packages/rbac/tests/integration/write.test.ts)). 11 cases. Three scope-guard cases on `assignRole` : SYSADMIN with a non-null orgId rejects (it's platform-tier only), ADMIN with a null orgId rejects (it's org-tier only), and a custom role granted at the wrong org rejects. Two happy-path acceptance cases. Three on the `alreadyActive` idempotency contract : a re-grant returns `alreadyActive=true` with the same assignmentId, no `rbac.role-assigned` event re-emission on the retry (the load-bearing "don't double-fire notification fan-out on a retried mutation" guarantee), and the first-grant event payload shape (assignmentId, userId, organizationId, roleId, roleKey, grantedById, reason, occurredAt). Two on `revokeRole` : full-payload `rbac.role-revoked` emission + the no-op behaviour when revoking a non-existent assignment. Captures via the in-memory event bus through `on("*", …)` ; `_resetHandlersForTesting` in `beforeEach` clears subscribers between specs.
  - **Net effect.** The two highest-risk surfaces left on the test-plan list now have direct integration coverage. Combined with the prior batches : the authorization-decision path (rbac/read), the role lifecycle (rbac/role-crud + rbac/write), the org provisioning (organizations/bootstrap + invites + data), the webhook delivery state machine (webhooks/data), the user lifecycle + notifications + auth + common-layer helpers all enforce a CI gate on every push. Full typecheck across 15 packages stays green.

- 2026-05-08: Test backfill against the test plan. Three new suites land against gaps the plan flagged ; the authorization + structured-logging surfaces now have direct coverage instead of relying on the integration suites that exercise them as a side effect.
  - **`@monark/common` logger redaction** ([packages/common/tests/log.test.ts](monark/app/packages/common/tests/log.test.ts)). Drives a real pino instance configured with the production `REDACT_PATHS` against a captured Writable destination, asserts each credential-flavoured key (password / token / secret / cookie / hash variants) renders as `[REDACTED]`, and pins the `*.field` wildcard's "one level deep, structured payloads only" semantics so a future contributor doesn't add a root-level credential and assume it's redacted. Also covers the request-header path (`req.headers.authorization`, `cookie`, `set-cookie`, `x-supabase-auth`, `x-api-key`, `x-csrf-token`, `proxy-authorization`) and a "lookalike key" guard (`passwordless`, `tokenized`, `secretly` pass through unchanged because the matcher is exact, not substring). `REDACT_PATHS` exported from `log.ts` so the test can validate the production list directly.
  - **`@monark/common` tRPC code mapping** ([packages/common/tests/trpc.test.ts](monark/app/packages/common/tests/trpc.test.ts) + [packages/common/src/trpc.ts](monark/app/packages/common/src/trpc.ts)). Locks in `APP_TO_TRPC_CODE` : `not_found → NOT_FOUND`, `unauthorized → UNAUTHORIZED`, `forbidden → FORBIDDEN`, `validation_error → BAD_REQUEST`, `conflict → CONFLICT`, plus the "every domain error class's code lands on the map" snapshot guard so a new AppError subclass can't ship without a corresponding tRPC code. The mapping table is exported (was a private const) ; the middleware itself stays the canonical source. Tested directly against the table because vite-node's per-relative-path module caching breaks `instanceof AppError` across the test/source boundary — production runs through a single import path and works correctly. The full middleware behavior is implicitly covered by every integration suite that throws an AppError through a real tRPC procedure.
  - **`@monark/rbac` read-layer integration** ([packages/rbac/tests/integration/read.test.ts](monark/app/packages/rbac/tests/integration/read.test.ts)). The two load-bearing authorization short-circuits get direct coverage : (1) SYSADMIN holders implicitly hold every permission across every org regardless of the requested orgId, (2) ADMIN holders implicitly hold every permission within their org and don't leak across org boundaries. Also covers `hasPermission` falling through to `RolePermission` rows for custom roles + non-admin users, the `ValidationError` thrown for malformed permission keys, `hasRoleKey` + the "ADMIN check requires orgId" programming-error gate, `getUserRoles` deduping across platform/org tier, `isLastAdmin` for the "block last admin leaving" surface, and `adminAssignmentSummary` for both SYSADMIN + ADMIN paths. 14 cases across 7 describe blocks, runs against the shared Postgres testcontainer.
  - **Deferred** : `services/api` integration suite. The `server.ts` boot has top-level side effects (registers permissions / event types / kinds + calls `app.listen()` at import time) that need a `createApp()` extraction before integration tests can boot the express app without firing those side effects. Tracked as a follow-up ; meanwhile the cron-auth helper + `processExpiredDeletions` data layer are already covered by the `cron-auth.test.ts` unit + the `@monark/auth` integration suite.
  - **Net effect.** `pnpm typecheck` clean across all 15 packages + services. New tests : 4 logger cases + 7 tRPC-mapping cases (run on every push via `pnpm test:coverage`) + 14 rbac-read cases (run on every push via `pnpm test:integration`). The webhook + rbac + auth + organizations + users + notifications + webhooks integration suites + the email-shell snapshot suite + the new logger / tRPC / rbac-read suites form the test-plan target shape : every load-bearing data-layer + auth-decision path has a CI-enforced regression gate, and the AppError → tRPC mapping a client branches on can't silently lose its code wiring.

- 2026-05-08: Seeded e2e test users on CI. The six remaining gated specs (account-deletion, password-change, signin-happy-path, admin-rbac-create-role, admin-users-invite, admin-webhooks) graduate from "skipped" to "executed" on every push.
  - **New `tools/seed-e2e-users.ts`** ([tools/seed-e2e-users.ts](monark/app/tools/seed-e2e-users.ts)). Provisions `e2e-user@monark.test` (regular) + `e2e-admin@monark.test` (built-in ADMIN scoped to the singleton org). Each pass : (1) `auth.admin.createUser` with `email_confirm: true` so the user can sign in without bouncing through verification ; (2) Prisma `User.upsert` keyed on the auth UUID so the row landings line up with everything that joins on `User.id` ; (3) for the admin user, `assignRole` against the `role_admin_builtin` row scoped to the singleton org. Idempotent at every layer — re-running on a healthy install is a no-op (existing auth user gets a password refresh ; existing User row gets the same upsert ; existing role assignment short-circuits via `alreadyActive`). Bootstraps the singleton org along the way so the ADMIN grant has somewhere to scope to ; the api process would do this at boot anyway, but Playwright doesn't start the api until after the seed step.
  - **CI workflow** ([.github/workflows/ci.yml](monark/app/.github/workflows/ci.yml)). New "Seed e2e test users" step in the `e2e` job, between `db:migrate` and `Install Playwright browsers`. The seed credentials live on the job-level `env` block — deterministic test passwords (the runner only listens on localhost and the stack is torn down at job end) strong enough to clear the password-rule validator.
  - **Net effect.** With this + the prior `E2E_FULL_STACK=1` + `INBUCKET_URL` flip, every gated e2e spec now actually runs : signup-confirm + forgot-password (no-seed flows) and the six seeded-user specs. Real regression in any of those — admin role grant via the UI, password-change with TOTP, account-deletion grace lockdown, webhook end-to-end delivery, etc. — fails the build instead of leaking to staging.

- 2026-05-08: Test confidence + CI scope upgrade. Three concrete landings against the test-plan : a new integration suite for the highest-risk package without one, the existing e2e specs for no-seed flows actually running in CI, and integration suites brought into the verify job so DB-layer regressions stop slipping past the gate.
  - **`@monark/webhooks` integration suite** ([packages/webhooks/tests/integration/data.test.ts](monark/app/packages/webhooks/tests/integration/data.test.ts)). Mirrors the existing rbac / users / organizations / notifications shape : Postgres testcontainer via `@monark/test-utils/global-setup`, fileParallelism off, one-org seed in `beforeAll`, table truncate in `afterEach`. Covers the load-bearing data-layer paths : create / find / list / update / delete endpoint round-trip ; rotate-secret swaps the hash cleanly ; enqueueDeliveries swallows P2002 on duplicate idempotency key (the "retry the source mutation, don't double-deliver" guarantee) ; listPendingDueDeliveries respects the nextAttemptAt cutoff ; markDelivery{Succeeded, Retry, Failed} update the consecutive-failure counter the auto-disable threshold reads ; disableEndpointForFailures + recordAttempt persist the row state the worker depends on. New `vitest.integration.config.ts` + `@monark/test-utils` workspace devDep + `test:integration` script.
  - **CI verify job now runs integration tests** ([.github/workflows/ci.yml:56-65](monark/app/.github/workflows/ci.yml#L56-L65) + [turbo.json](monark/app/turbo.json) + [package.json](monark/app/package.json)). New `test:integration` turbo task + root script ; verify CI step runs `pnpm test:integration` after `pnpm test:coverage`. Adds ~25 s to verify (5 packages × ~5 s testcontainer boot, amortised across each package's spec body). Docker is available on `ubuntu-latest` without setup, so no infrastructure changes ; packages without an integration config `--passWithNoTests` and stay cheap.
  - **E2E specs that don't need a seeded user now run on CI** ([.github/workflows/ci.yml e2e job env](monark/app/.github/workflows/ci.yml#L113-L122)). Sets `E2E_FULL_STACK=1` + `INBUCKET_URL=http://localhost:54324` at the e2e job level, so `signup-confirm.spec.ts` (signup → poll Inbucket → click confirmation link → land authenticated) and `forgot-password.spec.ts` (request reset → poll → click → set new password) graduate from "skipped" to "executed". Specs that _also_ gate on `E2E_USER_PASSWORD` / `E2E_ADMIN_PASSWORD` (account-deletion, password-change, signin-happy-path, admin-rbac-create-role, admin-users-invite, admin-webhooks) stay skipped until the next round wires seeded test users.
  - **Coverage thresholds deferred + documented** ([packages/rbac/vitest.config.ts](monark/app/packages/rbac/vitest.config.ts)). The blocker : each package's data / read / write files are exercised by the integration config, not the unit one. Unit-only coverage on rbac is ~8 %, so any meaningful threshold on `test:coverage` would break CI. The fix is to merge unit + integration coverage in one run ; tracked as a follow-up. Comments on the rbac config now explain the deferral so the next reader doesn't repeat the threshold-then-revert dance.
  - **Net effect.** The verify CI job grows by one step that catches every Prisma-layer regression in users / organizations / rbac / notifications / webhooks / auth. Two real e2e flows (signup-confirm, forgot-password) start running on every push, so a regression in the email-confirm or password-reset path breaks the build instead of leaking to staging. The webhook delivery state machine has its first end-to-end-against-Postgres coverage. Full typecheck across every package + service stays green.

- 2026-05-08: Webhook endpoints get an operator-facing `name`. The URL is no longer the row identity ; a short label drives the list, the page header, the breadcrumb, and any future filtering UI.
  - **Schema** : new `name TEXT NOT NULL` column on `WebhookEndpoint` ([packages/db/prisma/schema.prisma](monark/app/packages/db/prisma/schema.prisma)). Migration [20260508100000_add_webhook_name](monark/app/packages/db/prisma/migrations/20260508100000_add_webhook_name/migration.sql) lands the column in three steps so existing rows survive : add nullable, backfill from URL (strip scheme, trim trailing slash, cap at 80 chars), promote to NOT NULL. Defensive fallback to `Webhook <id-prefix>` for any degenerate row that ended up empty after the regex.
  - **Server** : new `hasSysadminAssignment` data helper + `rbac.isSysadmin` tRPC procedure ([packages/rbac/src/server/{data.ts,index.ts}](monark/app/packages/rbac/src/server)) — distinct from `isAdmin` which lights up for org-tier admins too. `webhooks.create` now requires `name: 1..80 chars` ; `webhooks.update` accepts an optional name patch ; the `webhook.endpoint-updated` event's `changed` enum picked up `"name"` ([packages/webhooks/src/contracts/events.ts](monark/app/packages/webhooks/src/contracts/events.ts) + [packages/webhooks/src/server/{router.ts,data.ts}](monark/app/packages/webhooks/src/server)).
  - **Frontend** :
    - Webhook editor ([webhook-editor.tsx](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/admin/webhooks/webhook-editor.tsx>)) gains a name input as the first field after Scope, dirty-tracks it for the save bar, and uses it as the page-header title (`Edit {name}` ; falls back to "Edit endpoint" while loading).
    - WebhooksManager list ([webhooks-manager.tsx](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/admin/webhooks/webhooks-manager.tsx>)) flips the row identity : name is the headline, URL drops to a mono `text-xs` subtitle. Search now matches across name + URL + description.
    - Deliveries header ([deliveries-list.tsx](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/admin/webhooks/[id]/deliveries/deliveries-list.tsx>)) reuses the same name-driven title for tab-switch parity.
    - AppBar breadcrumb ([app-bar-breadcrumb.tsx](monark/app/services/web/src/components/app-bar-breadcrumb.tsx)) gains a `WebhookNameCrumb` dispatch for `/admin/webhooks/<id>` ; previously the cuid rendered raw.
    - Scope row in the editor is now sysadmin-only (org-admins never had an alternative to "Organization-scoped" — hiding the read-only field removes a useless row).
  - **i18n** : new `admin.webhooks.editor.{nameLabel,namePlaceholder,nameHint,nameInvalid,editTitleFallback}` ; `editTitle` now interpolates `{name}`. Search placeholder copy updated to mention name. (en + fr.)
  - **Handoff CLI steps** (run from `monark/app/`) :
    1. `pnpm --filter @monark/db prisma migrate deploy` — applies the migration. Existing endpoints are backfilled automatically.
    2. `pnpm --filter @monark/db prisma generate` — refreshes the Prisma client. (Already done in this batch ; only re-run on a clean checkout.)
    3. Restart `pnpm dev` so the api process picks up the new schema.

- 2026-05-07: Detail-page UX refinement pass. Builds on the prior card-less standardization with a shared page header + an editable user banner + an explicit save model on org settings, plus a Danger-Zone card wrapper that lifts irreversible actions out of the rest of the page.
  - **Shared `<PageHeader>` primitive** ([services/web/src/components/page-header.tsx](monark/app/services/web/src/components/page-header.tsx)). Renders the page-level `h1` + optional subtitle + a back-button affordance that lives in the left gutter on `xl+` viewports (absolute-positioned so the title's vertical anchor doesn't shift between sibling pages) and falls back to an inline link on narrow screens. Adds a bottom `<Separator />` by default. Used by `/admin/webhooks/[id]` (editor + tabs nav + deliveries list + delivery detail), `/admin/rbac/roles/[id]`, `/admin/organizations/[id]`. `<AccountPageHeader>` now delegates to it so heading hierarchy is uniform.
  - **Shared `<UserBanner>` hero** ([services/web/src/components/user-banner.tsx](monark/app/services/web/src/components/user-banner.tsx)). Full-bleed banner image (or brand gradient) with a `from-transparent from-50% to-background` fade, avatar + display name + optional badges aligned to the banner's bottom edge. Mirrors the user-menu drawer's identity treatment so the same visual identity treatment lands in both places. Optional `edit` prop wires up file pickers + Replace/Remove dropdowns + hover overlays so the banner can be the editing surface itself ; the caller stays in charge of upload mutations + crop dialogs (self-service crops 1:1 / 3:1 ; admin uploads skip the crop step). Both `/account/profile` (ProfileSection) and `/admin/users/[id]` (AdminProfileForm) now render UserBanner as their hero ; the embedded banner editors that used to live inside those forms are gone, and AdminProfileForm's redundant "Profile / Edit display info" `PageSection` wrapper is dropped.
  - **`/admin/organizations/[id]` switches from on-blur autosave to an explicit Save / Cancel footer** ([organization-detail.tsx](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/admin/organizations/[id]/organization-detail.tsx>)). Save sits right-most ; Cancel sits to its left. Both are disabled until at least one field actually changed (dirty-check vs. the source row). The mutation only ships fields that diverge from the baseline, so audit-log entries don't churn for round-trips. New `admin.organizations.detail.{save,saving,cancel,backAria}` i18n keys.
  - **Shared `<DangerCard>` + `<DangerRow>`** ([services/web/src/components/danger-card.tsx](monark/app/services/web/src/components/danger-card.tsx)). Card-bordered wrapper for irreversible actions ; tone variant flips between red (`danger`, default) and amber (`warning`, used for the in-grace-cancel surface). Each row inside is a `<DangerRow>` with title + description on the left and the action button on the right. Applied to the webhook editor's rotate-secret + delete pair, `/account/danger` (delete account / cancel scheduled deletion), and `/admin/users/[id]`'s Danger Zone (request deletion + hard delete + grace-period cancel). New `account.danger.{deleteDescription,cancelDescription}` + `admin.users.dangerZone.cancelDescription` i18n keys.
  - **`/account/profile` skips `<AccountPageHeader>` entirely** since the UserBanner serves as its hero. Other account tabs (security, notifications, danger) still render AccountPageHeader.
  - **i18n cleanup**. Em-dashes scrubbed from prose across en.json + fr.json (replaced with `;` / `:` / `,` per context, per the project's prose-style rule). Org detail subtitle drops the "Edits are saved on blur" copy now that the form gates on Save.
  - **Net effect.** `pnpm typecheck` clean. The same heading hierarchy + visual rhythm now anchors every detail page across the admin + account surfaces ; destructive actions are visually carded so they read as distinct from the editing surface above them ; the user-identity treatment is one component everywhere it appears.

- 2026-05-07: Visual standardization across the admin + account detail surfaces. The webhook detail page's "card-less" feel is now the canonical pattern : page-level `h1` + subtitle, sections rendered as `h2` with `<Separator />` between them, no `<Card>` wrappers around section content.
  - **Shared `<PageSection>` primitive** ([services/web/src/components/page-section.tsx](monark/app/services/web/src/components/page-section.tsx)). Renders a real `<h2>` (Card components emitted `<div>`s, breaking screen-reader hierarchy) + optional subtitle + header-row action slot + a `tone` variant (`default` / `danger` / `warning`) for color-accented sections like Danger Zone. Replaces ~12 ad-hoc `<Card className="bg-transparent shadow-none">` instances across the admin + account surfaces.
  - **Shared `<AccountPageHeader>`** ([services/web/src/app/(authed)/account/account-page-header.tsx](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/account/account-page-header.tsx>)). Renders the page-level `h1` + subtitle for `/account/{profile,security,notifications,danger}`, sourcing the title from `account.tabs.<tab>` (matches the sidebar) and the subtitle from new `account.subtitles.<tab>` keys. Tone variant pins the title color for Danger Zone.
  - **Refactored pages** :
    - `/admin/webhooks/[id]` ([webhook-editor.tsx](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/admin/webhooks/webhook-editor.tsx>)) — split into "Endpoint" (URL / scope / description / status) + "Event subscriptions" PageSections with separators ; the action footer becomes its own delimited row. The user pointed to this layout as the reference look.
    - `/admin/rbac/roles/[id]` ([role-editor.tsx](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/admin/rbac/role-editor.tsx>)) — replaced the `rounded-lg border` identity grid + permission-block borders with PageSections "Identity" + "Permissions". The lock badge for built-in ADMIN moves into the PageSection's action slot.
    - `/admin/users/[id]` ([user-detail.tsx](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/admin/users/[id]/user-detail.tsx>) + 5 sub-components) — Profile, Identity, Roles, Account actions, Notifications, Danger zone subsections all converted to PageSections with separators between them. Danger zone uses `tone="danger"`.
    - `/admin/organizations/[id]` ([organization-detail.tsx](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/admin/organizations/[id]/organization-detail.tsx>)) — single PageSection ; loading + error states drop their Card wrappers for a flat skeleton row.
    - `/account/profile` — page renders the AccountPageHeader (h1) ; ProfileSection drops its own Card and renders content directly under the page header (single block, no extra h2 needed for one section).
    - `/account/security` — page renders AccountPageHeader (h1) ; the four sub-sections (Email, Password, TOTP, Trusted devices) each become a PageSection with a `<Separator />` between them. Heading hierarchy is h1 → h2 × 4.
    - `/account/notifications` — same shape : AccountPageHeader + a single PageSection.
    - `/account/danger` — AccountPageHeader with `tone="danger"` ; the "grace period" + the deletion-not-yet-pending paths both render through PageSection (the former with `tone="warning"`, the latter with `tone="danger"`).
  - **i18n.** Added `account.subtitles.{profile,security,notifications,danger}` (en + fr), `admin.webhooks.editor.{endpointSectionTitle,endpointSectionSubtitle,subsSectionSubtitle}`, `admin.rbac.editor.{identitySectionTitle,identitySectionSubtitle,permissionsSectionSubtitle}`. Existing `account.{profile,email,password,totp,trustedDevices,notifications,danger}.title/subtitle` keys are reused as section h2 + subtitle copy.
  - **Net effect.** Every admin + account detail page now follows the same pattern : one h1 page header + N PageSections separated by `<Separator />` rules. No more bordered card boxes around every settings group ; heading-hierarchy is correct for screen readers + visual scanning. `pnpm typecheck` clean across all 15 packages.

- 2026-05-07: Email + in-app alerts for permanent webhook delivery failures + endpoint auto-disable. Operators now get notified when a receiver is broken instead of having to poll the admin UI.
  - **Two new notification kinds** registered in [packages/notifications/src/server/register-core-kinds.ts](monark/app/packages/notifications/src/server/register-core-kinds.ts) :
    - `webhooks.delivery-permanently-failed` — fires when a single delivery exhausts its 5 retries (`permanent: true` on the existing `webhook.delivery-failed` event). SECURITY category, EMAIL + IN_APP, default-on, **opt-out**-able from the prefs UI since a flapping receiver could otherwise spam the inbox.
    - `webhooks.endpoint-auto-disabled` — fires when an endpoint hits 5 consecutive failures and the worker auto-disables it. SECURITY category, EMAIL + IN_APP, **`requiredEmail: true`** (cannot be opted out) — this is the actionable "your endpoint is dead" signal that operators always want.
  - **Templates** under [packages/notifications/src/templates/webhooks/](monark/app/packages/notifications/src/templates/webhooks/) — both en + fr, with subject + html + text + inapp slots. The HTML mirrors the brand chrome of existing security templates (orange CTA button, neutral table for the metadata rows, red highlight on the error reason). The CTAs link to `/admin/webhooks/<id>/deliveries` (delivery-failed) and `/admin/webhooks/<id>` (auto-disabled). Two new template vars derived in [enrich.ts](monark/app/packages/notifications/src/server/enrich.ts) : `webhookDeliveriesLink`, `webhookEndpointLink`, plus `failureLimit` from `WEBHOOK_DELIVERY_FAILURE_LIMIT`.
  - **Fan-out subscribers** in [packages/notifications/src/server/subscribers/index.ts](monark/app/packages/notifications/src/server/subscribers/index.ts) :
    - `webhook.delivery-failed` (with `permanent: true` filter) → fan out to org admins (org-scoped endpoint) or sysadmins (platform-tier endpoint).
    - `webhook.endpoint-disabled-after-failures` → same fan-out.
    - The dispatcher's existing 60s dedupe window collapses near-simultaneous failures of the same delivery so a synchronized retry cluster doesn't double-send.
  - **`organizationId` + `endpointUrl` on delivery events** ([packages/webhooks/src/contracts/events.ts](monark/app/packages/webhooks/src/contracts/events.ts)). Added to `WebhookDeliverySucceededEvent` and `WebhookDeliveryFailedEvent` so subscribers can route to the right operator group + render the URL in the email without an extra DB roundtrip per event. The worker populates both from the existing `delivery.endpoint` join.
  - **Two new rbac data helpers** ([packages/rbac/src/server/data.ts](monark/app/packages/rbac/src/server/data.ts)) : `listOrgAdminUserIds(orgId)` returns the active built-in `ADMIN` holders for one org ; `listSysadminUserIds()` returns the active platform-tier `SYSADMIN` holders. Returns user ids only — caller hydrates User rows themselves. Re-exported from `@monark/rbac/server`.
  - **Dependency**: `@monark/notifications` now depends on `@monark/webhooks` (for the event type imports + `WEBHOOK_DELIVERY_FAILURE_LIMIT` constant). Direction is one-way ; webhooks doesn't depend on notifications, no cycle.
  - **Effect.** A webhook receiver that's been down long enough to exhaust retries on a delivery generates one email per qualifying admin, plus a follow-up "endpoint auto-disabled" email when the consecutive-failure cap hits. Admins recover by fixing the receiver and re-enabling the endpoint from `/admin/webhooks/<id>` ; the failure counter resets on the next successful delivery.
  - `pnpm typecheck` stays clean across all 15 packages.

- 2026-05-07: Webhook secret store defaults to file-backed in non-production + delivery-detail event-type rendering polish.
  - **File-backed by default in dev.** [packages/webhooks/src/server/secret-store.ts](monark/app/packages/webhooks/src/server/secret-store.ts) auto-engages a `FileBackedStore` when `NODE_ENV !== "production"`, defaulting to `<api cwd>/.cache/monark-webhook-secrets.json`. Means secrets survive `pnpm dev` restarts out-of-the-box ; previously the in-memory store cleared on every restart and every endpoint failed with "no plaintext secret available" until rotated. Override path with `MONARK_DEV_WEBHOOK_SECRETS_FILE`. Production still defaults to in-memory ; the env var is honored there too but logs a warning since plaintext-on-disk is rarely the right production answer.
  - **Gitignored.** Added `.cache/` to [.gitignore](monark/app/.gitignore) so the per-process secret file doesn't leak into version control.
  - **Existing endpoints need a one-time rotate.** Endpoints created before this change have no plaintext stored anywhere. Visit `/admin/webhooks/<id>` and click "Rotate secret" once ; the rotation lands the new plaintext in the file-backed store immediately and every delivery from then on signs cleanly.
  - **Delivery detail event-type styling.** [services/web/src/app/(authed)/admin/webhooks/[id]/deliveries/[deliveryId]/delivery-detail.tsx](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/admin/webhooks/[id]/deliveries/[deliveryId]/delivery-detail.tsx>) now renders the event type in `font-mono` (signaling it's a technical identifier, not a missing translation) and shows the registered description from `webhooks.listEventTypes` as a subtitle below. Operators get both the wire-level type (`user.signed-in`) and the operator-friendly meaning ("A user authenticated successfully…") in one glance. The deliveries list rows already used mono ; this aligns the detail page header with that convention.

- 2026-05-07: Auto-membership for single-tenant deploys + webhook fallback. The morning's three-rule routing fix landed correctly but exposed a deeper gap : `OrganizationMembership` was only ever populated by the invite-acceptance path, so direct sign-ups in single-tenant deploys had no membership row. The webhook subscriber's user-tied membership rule needed something to look up ; users had nothing.
  - **`ensureSingletonMembership(userId)`** ([packages/organizations/src/server/auto-membership.ts](monark/app/packages/organizations/src/server/auto-membership.ts)) idempotently upserts an `OrganizationMembership` row for the singleton org when : (a) the multi-tenant flag is OFF, (b) exactly one non-deleted org exists, and (c) the user has no active membership. Emits `organization.member-joined` on a fresh upsert so the webhook outbox sees the join with the same shape as an invite-driven one. Members an admin explicitly removed (`leftAt` set) are NOT auto-rejoined — sign-in won't undo a removal silently.
  - **`registerOrganizationsSubscribers()`** wires the helper to `user.signed-up` + `user.signed-in` on the bus. Registered at api boot before `registerWebhookSubscribers()` so the derived `member-joined` event reaches the webhook outbox in the same emit pass. Idempotent ; calling twice is a no-op.
  - **Webhook subscriber single-tenant fallback** ([packages/webhooks/src/server/subscribers.ts](monark/app/packages/webhooks/src/server/subscribers.ts)) — when the membership lookup returns empty for a user-tied event AND there's exactly one non-deleted org, the subscriber treats the singleton as the implicit org and routes to its endpoints. Covers (a) brand-new accounts whose first sign-in fires before the auto-membership upsert lands, and (b) deploys that pre-date this CHANGELOG entry where every existing user has no membership row. New `findOnlySingletonOrgId()` helper in [packages/webhooks/src/server/data.ts](monark/app/packages/webhooks/src/server/data.ts) does the lookup with a `take: 2` so it returns null for multi-org deploys without scanning the full table.
  - **`@monark/organizations` now depends on `@monark/auth`** for the type imports of `UserSignedUpEvent` / `UserSignedInEvent` (used by the `on<E>(...)` generics so the handler narrows the event payload). Direction is one-way ; auth has never depended on organizations, no cycle.
  - **Effect.** Sign in as any user once and the `OrganizationMembership` table populates. Webhooks subscribed to `user.signed-in` / `user.signed-out` (or any user-tied event) deliver to org-scoped endpoints from the next emit forward. The fallback covers the very first sign-in before the upsert commits.
  - **Backfill (optional).** Existing single-tenant deploys with users predating this change can either let the auto-membership subscriber backfill on each user's next sign-in, or run a one-shot script :
    ```ts
    import { getDb } from "@monark/db";
    import { findOnlyActiveOrganization } from "@monark/organizations/server";
    const db = getDb();
    const org = await findOnlyActiveOrganization();
    if (org) {
      await db.organizationMembership.createMany({
        data: (await db.user.findMany({ select: { id: true } })).map((u) => ({
          userId: u.id,
          organizationId: org.id,
        })),
        skipDuplicates: true,
      });
    }
    ```
  - `pnpm typecheck` stays clean across all 15 packages.

- 2026-05-07: Webhook routing fix for user-tied events. Bug : `user.signed-in`, `user.signed-out`, and every other event whose payload carries a `userId` but no `organizationId` were silently dropped for org-scoped endpoints — the previous routing filter only matched on `event.organizationId === endpoint.organizationId`, so events with no org id never reached non-platform endpoints. Most operators create org-scoped endpoints (the picker's default in single-tenant deploys), so user-tied events never fired.
  - **Three-rule routing** in [packages/webhooks/src/server/subscribers.ts](monark/app/packages/webhooks/src/server/subscribers.ts) replaces the single-rule org filter :
    1. **Platform-tier** (`organizationId = null`) — receives every match. Unchanged.
    2. **Direct org match** — event payload's `organizationId` equals the endpoint's. Unchanged.
    3. **User-tied membership match** (NEW) — event has a `userId` but no `organizationId` ; subscriber looks up the user's active `OrganizationMembership` rows (excluding `leftAt`) and fans out to every org-scoped endpoint whose org the user belongs to.
  - **Lazy membership lookup.** The new DB roundtrip only fires when there's at least one org-scoped endpoint that needs it AND the event is user-tied. Platform-only or org-direct events skip the lookup entirely. `findUserMemberOrgIds(userId)` lives in [packages/webhooks/src/server/data.ts](monark/app/packages/webhooks/src/server/data.ts) ; the subscriber caches the result for the life of one event so multiple matching endpoints share one query.
  - **Effect.** Org-scoped endpoints subscribed to `user.signed-in` / `user.signed-out` / `user.password-changed` / `totp.enabled` / `notification.created` / etc. now receive deliveries for every member of their org. Former members (rows with `leftAt` set) are excluded — a user's later events don't leak to an org they've left.
  - Routing semantics documented in [packages/webhooks/README.md § Routing semantics](monark/app/packages/webhooks/README.md#routing-semantics). `pnpm typecheck` stays clean across all 15 packages.

- 2026-05-07: Webhook URL validator allows http:// for loopback / RFC 1918 hosts in development. Production stays https-only.
  - [packages/webhooks/src/server/router.ts](monark/app/packages/webhooks/src/server/router.ts) replaces `assertHttps()` with `assertSafeUrl()` that parses the URL and applies the same private-host shape the api's CORS layer already uses for cross-origin browser calls in dev (`localhost`, `127.0.0.1`, `[::1]`, `10.x`, `172.16-31.x`, `192.168.x`). Public hosts over plaintext http are always rejected ; production never accepts http regardless of host.
  - Client-side regex in [services/web/src/app/(authed)/admin/webhooks/webhook-editor.tsx](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/admin/webhooks/webhook-editor.tsx>) loosened to `^https?://` (basic shape check) ; server is the source of truth on which combinations are allowed. The toast forwards the server error so operators see "URL must be https:// in production" or "http:// is allowed in development only for loopback or private (RFC 1918) hosts" exactly.
  - i18n urlHint in `en` + `fr` mentions the dev allowance. URL scheme rules documented in [packages/webhooks/README.md § URL scheme rules](monark/app/packages/webhooks/README.md#url-scheme-rules) and the smoke check in [docs/technical-documentation/webhook-secret-resolver.md](monark/app/docs/technical-documentation/webhook-secret-resolver.md) now uses `pnpm webhook-receiver` on `http://127.0.0.1:4123/hook`.

- 2026-05-07: Webhook subscription picker rewrite + mock receiver + e2e + secret-store overhaul. Closes the operator-friction gap on the morning's webhook admin UI.
  - **Event-type registry as the 6th extension point.** [packages/common/src/event-registry.ts](monark/app/packages/common/src/event-registry.ts) ships `registerEventTypes(module, types)` + `listEventTypesByModule()`. Each domain module registers its events with operator-facing descriptions at api boot ([packages/auth/src/server/event-types.ts](monark/app/packages/auth/src/server/event-types.ts), [packages/feature-flags/src/server/event-types.ts](monark/app/packages/feature-flags/src/server/event-types.ts), notifications / organizations / rbac / users mirrors). Wired in [services/api/src/server.ts](monark/app/services/api/src/server.ts) next to the other `register*` calls. Total : 31 event types registered ; `webhook.*` events intentionally skipped because the wildcard subscriber filters them to avoid recursion.
  - **`webhooks.listEventTypes` tRPC procedure.** Returns the merged registry grouped by module, gated on the rbac `webhooks.read` permission. Powers the new picker.
  - **Subscription picker** ([services/web/src/app/(authed)/admin/webhooks/subscription-picker.tsx](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/admin/webhooks/subscription-picker.tsx>)). Replaces the previous free-text + advanced-mode rows with a categorized expansion-panel surface that mirrors the RBAC role-editor's permission picker exactly : bordered container, one Collapsible per module with a tri-state header checkbox + chevron + `selected/total` count chip, individual event rows as bordered labels with mono key + plain-language description. Tri-state behavior writes a single prefix subscription (`rbac.`) when every event in the module shares a common dotted prefix ; falls back to N exact subscriptions when they don't (the auth module's mixed `user.` / `trusted-device.` / `totp.` events). Pre-existing custom subscriptions that aren't in the registry surface as removable chips at the top so operators can clean them up. Advanced free-text mode removed entirely — the picker covers every legitimate case.
  - **`tools/webhook-receiver.ts` mock receiver.** Standalone Node HTTP server that captures incoming POSTs at `/hook`, exposes the captures as JSON via `/inbox`, and optionally validates HMAC signatures (`--secret <plaintext>`) or fails the first request once (`--fail-once`) for retry-loop testing. Run via `pnpm webhook-receiver --port 4123` for manual smoke testing while developing webhook receivers ; the e2e harness boots it as a child process.
  - **e2e spec** [services/web/tests/e2e/admin-webhooks.spec.ts](monark/app/services/web/tests/e2e/admin-webhooks.spec.ts). Boots the receiver in `beforeAll`, signs in as the seeded admin, drives /admin/webhooks → New endpoint → ticks the `rbac` group tri-state → Create → triggers `rbac.role-created` by creating a custom role → polls the receiver's `/inbox` until the matching capture lands → asserts the `Webhook-Signature` header is `v1=<hex>`, `Webhook-Delivery-Idempotency-Key` is present, and the body's `data.roleKey` matches the created role. Cleanup deletes the role + the endpoint so re-runs are idempotent. Helper at [services/web/tests/e2e/helpers/webhook-receiver.ts](monark/app/services/web/tests/e2e/helpers/webhook-receiver.ts) handles the child-process spawn + healthz poll + waitForCapture utility. Gated behind `E2E_FULL_STACK=1`.
  - **Secret-store refactor.** [packages/webhooks/src/server/secret-store.ts](monark/app/packages/webhooks/src/server/secret-store.ts) replaces the read-only `setWebhookSecretResolver()` with a `SecretStore` interface (put / get / delete) plus a default in-memory implementation that the router populates on every create + rotate (and clears on delete). Means out-of-the-box dev / single-process deploys work end-to-end without wiring an external secret store. An opt-in file-backed implementation (set `MONARK_DEV_WEBHOOK_SECRETS_FILE=<path>` outside production) survives `pnpm dev` restarts. Production deploys still swap the store via `setWebhookSecretStore()` for AWS Secrets Manager / Vault / GCP / Azure ; the seam is the same.
  - **Workspace effect.** Six new `register<Module>EventTypes()` helpers + the registry + the picker + the receiver + the e2e helper + the spec. ~30 new i18n keys in `en` + `fr` ; the previous `subsHint`, `advancedToggle`, `advancedAddRow`, etc. keys are dropped. `pnpm typecheck` stays clean across all 15 packages.
  - **Operator handoff** : `pnpm install`. To smoke-test manually : `pnpm webhook-receiver --port 4123` in one shell, `pnpm dev` in another, sign in as admin → /admin/webhooks → pick scope → New endpoint → URL = `http://127.0.0.1:4123/hook` → tick the `rbac` group → Create. Then create a custom role at /admin/rbac/roles/new ; the receiver's stdout shows the captured POST + `curl http://127.0.0.1:4123/inbox` returns the full payload. To run the e2e : `E2E_FULL_STACK=1 E2E_ADMIN_EMAIL=… E2E_ADMIN_PASSWORD=… pnpm test:e2e --grep webhooks`.

- 2026-05-07: `@monark/webhooks` admin UI under `/admin/webhooks`. Closes the deferred follow-up flagged in the morning's webhooks landing.
  - **Five pages mounted under `services/web/src/app/(authed)/admin/webhooks/`.**
    - `page.tsx` + [webhooks-manager.tsx](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/admin/webhooks/webhooks-manager.tsx>) — list per scope (org or platform-tier), URL/description search, status + recent-failure badges, single-tenant org picker collapses to the singleton.
    - `new/page.tsx` + [webhook-editor.tsx](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/admin/webhooks/webhook-editor.tsx>) — create form ; URL + description + per-subscription rows (event type + exact / prefix toggle). Server returns the plaintext signing secret once, surfaced in a green copy-once banner with a clipboard helper. Creation redirects to the edit page where the secret stays revealed until navigation.
    - `[id]/page.tsx` — edit form (same component, `mode="edit"`), surfaces a Rotate-secret button (confirmation modal, generates new plaintext + invalidates the previous one immediately) and a Delete button (typed confirmation against the URL).
    - `[id]/deliveries/page.tsx` + [deliveries-list.tsx](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/admin/webhooks/[id]/deliveries/deliveries-list.tsx>) — paged delivery history per endpoint with per-row status (delivered / pending / failed), attempt count, last-error snippet.
    - `[id]/deliveries/[deliveryId]/page.tsx` + [delivery-detail.tsx](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/admin/webhooks/[id]/deliveries/[deliveryId]/delivery-detail.tsx>) — full payload JSON-pretty-printed, per-attempt audit log (HTTP code or network error, duration ms, started timestamp), `Retry now` button that fires one synchronous attempt inline so the new row surfaces without waiting for the worker tick.
  - **Tab integration.** [admin-tabs.ts](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/admin/admin-tabs.ts>) gains a `webhooks` entry (4th tab, after rbac) wired to the existing AdminSidebar pattern. Tab label, status badges, button copy, error toasts, and confirmation dialogs are all i18n'd in `en` + `fr` under [messages/{en,fr}.json `admin.webhooks.*`](monark/app/services/web/src/messages/en.json) — 50+ new strings each.
  - **tRPC narrowing for the deeply-recursive `Json` payload.** Hit TS2589 ("type instantiation excessively deep") on the consumer side because `Prisma.JsonValue` is recursive and propagates through `webhooks.listDeliveries` / `webhooks.getDelivery` into the React component. Two fixes : `listDeliveriesForEndpoint` in the data layer now uses an explicit `select` that omits the `payload` column (the list view doesn't display it), and the `getDelivery` tRPC procedure casts `payload` to `unknown` at the wire boundary so the client carries a flat type while still allowing `JSON.stringify` to render the body. Workspace `pnpm typecheck` stays clean across all 15 packages.
  - **Operator handoff** : `pnpm install` + `pnpm typecheck`. To exercise : sign in as admin → `/admin/webhooks` → pick scope → "New endpoint" → fill URL + subscribe to e.g. `rbac.role-assigned` → Create → copy the returned secret → wire it through your secret resolver per [docs/technical-documentation/webhook-secret-resolver.md](monark/app/docs/technical-documentation/webhook-secret-resolver.md) → trigger a matching event from another part of the admin UI → watch the delivery land in `/admin/webhooks/<id>/deliveries`.

- 2026-05-07: Module-namespace foundation + `@monark/webhooks` core module. Five-phase rollout that opens core to extension by non-core modules without modifying core code, plus a generic webhook delivery system over the same event bus.
  - **Phase 1 — Module-namespace foundation.** Feature flags + RBAC permissions + notification kinds were each closed registries (`const FLAGS`, `const PERMISSIONS`, `const NOTIFICATION_KINDS`) where adding an entry meant editing a core file. Now they're runtime registries any module registers into at api boot.
    - Migration [20260507100000_add_module_to_flags_and_permissions](monark/app/packages/db/prisma/migrations/20260507100000_add_module_to_flags_and_permissions/migration.sql) adds a `module` column to `FeatureFlag`, `FeatureFlagOverride`, and `RolePermission`. Primary key on `FeatureFlag` becomes `(module, key)` ; uniqueness on `RolePermission` becomes `(roleId, module, permission)`. Backfill : `auth.*` flags get `module='auth'`, `tenancy.*` get `module='tenancy'` ; permission slugs lose their colon prefix (`org:update-settings` → `update-settings`), with the module derived from the prefix mapping (`org:` → `organizations` for the orgs-owned set + `rbac` for the rbac-owned set, etc.).
    - [packages/feature-flags/src/contracts/flags.ts](monark/app/packages/feature-flags/src/contracts/flags.ts) replaces the static `FLAGS` const with `registerFlags(module, flags)` + `isKnownFlag(dotted)` + `parseFlagKey(dotted)` + `listFlagDescriptors()`. Call sites still use the dotted form `"<module>.<key>"` ergonomically ; internal resolution parses to the (module, key) pair.
    - [packages/rbac/src/contracts/permissions.ts](monark/app/packages/rbac/src/contracts/permissions.ts) does the same for permissions ; `validatePermissionList` now returns parsed `{ module, key }[]` pairs, `hasPermission` / `requirePermission` accept the dotted form. Categories become loose strings so an extended module can introduce its own grouping.
    - Each owning module ships a `register<Module>FeatureFlags()` / `register<Module>Permissions()` helper. [services/api/src/server.ts](monark/app/services/api/src/server.ts) calls them in deterministic order at boot, before `syncFlagsToDatabase()`.
  - **Phase 2 — Notification kinds runtime registry.** [packages/notifications/src/contracts/registry.ts](monark/app/packages/notifications/src/contracts/registry.ts) drops the static `NOTIFICATION_KINDS` const + `TEMPLATES` map for `registerNotificationKind(kind, def, messages)`. The `NotificationDataRegistry` interface stays typed via TypeScript declaration merging so per-kind payloads remain checked at call sites. `registerCoreNotificationKinds()` registers the existing eight kinds (auth._ + account._) at api boot ; extended modules register their own with the same API.
  - **Phase 3 — User / Organization metadata sidecar.** Migration [20260507110000_add_user_org_metadata_sidecar](monark/app/packages/db/prisma/migrations/20260507110000_add_user_org_metadata_sidecar/migration.sql) adds `UserMetadata` + `OrganizationMetadata` tables keyed on `(parent_id, module, key)` with a `Json` value. Generic CRUD lives under `users.metadata.{list,get,set,delete}` and `organizations.metadata.{list,get,set,delete}` tRPC procedures, gated by `users.read-metadata-for-module-<x>` / `users.write-metadata-for-module-<x>` (and the orgs equivalent) permissions that an extended module registers itself. Lets a future `@monark/posts` attach per-user preferences without touching the core schema.
  - **Phase 4 — `@monark/webhooks` core module.** Operator-registered HTTP endpoints that receive a fan-out of every domain event the platform emits. Schema in migration [20260507120000_add_webhooks](monark/app/packages/db/prisma/migrations/20260507120000_add_webhooks/migration.sql) : `WebhookEndpoint` (URL + secretHash + status + per-org or platform scope), `WebhookSubscription` (event-type filter, exact or prefix), `WebhookDelivery` (the outbox row, idempotency-keyed), `WebhookDeliveryAttempt` (per-attempt audit). The wildcard subscriber listens on the new bus `*` channel ([packages/common/src/events.ts](monark/app/packages/common/src/events.ts)) so any module's events route through webhooks the moment they're in the manifest. The worker signs the body with HMAC-SHA-256 (`Webhook-Signature: v1=<hex>`), POSTs with a 10s timeout, retries with exponential backoff (5 attempts, 30s base, 6h cap), and auto-disables endpoints after 5 consecutive failures. `webhooksRouter` exposes endpoint CRUD + secret rotation + delivery inspection + manual retry under `webhooks.*` ; `/cron/sweep-webhook-deliveries` is the external fallback for the in-process worker. The admin UI on top of this surface is a deferred follow-up — tracked in [packages/webhooks/README.md](monark/app/packages/webhooks/README.md#deferred-follow-ups).
  - **Phase 5 — Documentation.** New [docs/technical-documentation/extensibility-contract.md](monark/app/docs/technical-documentation/extensibility-contract.md) codifies what core guarantees to extended modules and what extended modules must never touch. Per-module READMEs updated to reflect the runtime registries. Manifest gains `@monark/webhooks` at tier `core`.
  - **Workspace effect.** Three new migrations, one new core package (`@monark/webhooks`), one new schema column on three existing tables, one new wildcard channel on the event bus, six new permission slugs registered at boot. Migration is non-destructive — every existing row survives, FK and unique constraints widen rather than tighten. The `services/api` boot sequence gains five `register*` calls + the webhook worker + the cron sweep endpoint.
  - **Operator handoff** : `pnpm install`. `pnpm --filter @monark/db db:migrate:dev` to apply the three migrations in order. `pnpm gen` to regenerate `events.generated.ts` (already updated for `@monark/webhooks`) and the app router composition. `pnpm --filter api dev` and check the boot log for the new `webhook delivery worker started` line. The webhook secret resolver MUST be set via `setWebhookSecretResolver()` before the worker can sign requests — see [docs/technical-documentation/webhook-secret-resolver.md](docs/technical-documentation/webhook-secret-resolver.md) for three concrete backing-store recipes (env-var JSON, per-endpoint env vars, AWS Secrets Manager / Vault) ; until one is wired, every delivery records a "no plaintext secret available" error and the endpoint auto-disables after 5 consecutive failures.
  - **Codegen tools tolerate config-only modules.** `tools/gen-events.ts` and `tools/gen-routers.ts` previously aborted when a manifest module lacked `contracts/events.ts` or `server/index.ts` ; they now skip those modules silently. Lets `@monark/branding` (constants-only) live in the manifest for the tier check without forcing it to ship empty event / router stubs.

First-time `recognizeOrRegister` (cookie hash, IP, country, label parsing, DeviceSession creation when supabaseSessionId provided + skipped when null). Cookie recognition (re-uses row, refreshes country only on fresh signal, fresh row when cookie matches a different user, fresh row when previous was revoked, idempotent DeviceSession upsert). `listTrustedDevices` (lastSeenAt-DESC ordering, scoped to userId, excludes revoked). `findCurrentDeviceId` (match / null cookie / revoked-row null). `revokeTrustedDevice` (clears DeviceSession rows, no-op on wrong-user / already-revoked, idempotent revokedAt stamp). `revokeAllTrustedDevices` (returns count, scoped to user, returns 0 on empty).

Stubs `@monark/feature-flags/server` so `requiresTotpChallenge` doesn't depend on a flag-eval service. `getTotpStatus` (not enrolled / mid-enrollment / activated with remaining-codes count). `beginTotpEnrollment` (creates row, returns currentColor-themed SVG, encrypts secret round-trips, replaces unactivated re-enroll, rejects re-enroll when active). `confirmTotpEnrollment` (activates + mints 10 unique XXXX-XXXX-XXXX codes, rejects bad code, rejects no-enrollment, rejects already-active). `verifyTotpCode` (true / false / inactive). `verifyRecoveryCode` (consumes once, rejects unknown, uppercases input, returns false for non-enrolled). `regenerateRecoveryCodes` (replaces batch + invalidates old, rejects bad TOTP). `getRecoveryCodeStatus` + `acknowledgeRecoveryCodeUse` (use→unack→acknowledged round-trip). `disableTotp` (cascades RecoveryCode rows, rejects bad code). `requiresTotpChallenge` + `markDeviceTotpVerified` (untrusted vs verified device behaviour). `cleanupStaleTotpEnrollments` (drops only unactivated rows older than 24 h ; recent + activated rows survive).
    - [packages/auth/tests/integration/account-lifecycle.test.ts](monark/app/packages/auth/tests/integration/account-lifecycle.test.ts) — 7 cases. Stubs supabase admin's `deleteUser` at the import boundary so we don't hit a real Supabase. `hardDeleteUser` (anonymizes email + clears displayName + nulls avatarUrl, no-op when user missing, anonymizes our row even when supabase fails). `processExpiredDeletions` (only past-grace rows, idempotent on re-run via the `@monark.invalid` filter, supabase-fail still counted as success because the row flipped, returns zero counts on empty table).
  - **`@monark/notifications` integration suite.** [packages/notifications/tests/integration/dispatch.test.ts](monark/app/packages/notifications/tests/integration/dispatch.test.ts) — 14 cases. Forces SMTP to log-only mode (no `SMTP_URL` set) so `sendMail` short-circuits to a structured success ; the unit suite covers nodemailer interaction. `notify` happy path : two Notification rows (EMAIL + IN_APP) for `auth.password-changed`, deliveredAt stamp on EMAIL, body only set on IN_APP. Locale routing : FR user sees an accented FR subject. Dedupe window : same kind+payload within 60 s skipped, distinct payloads not deduped, never deduped across users. Soft-deleted recipient : IN_APP delivers, EMAIL skipped with reason `user-soft-deleted`. Opt-out semantics : non-required-email channel respects the `NotificationPreference` row, but `requiredEmail=true` overrides a disabled SECURITY/EMAIL pref. Unknown recipient returns the empty result without throwing. `notifyMany` aggregates deliveryIds across recipients. Prefs round-trip : `setPreference` upserts, `isChannelEnabled` reads, `resetPreferences` clears, no leak across users.
  - **`@monark/organizations` bootstrap integration.** [packages/organizations/tests/integration/bootstrap.test.ts](monark/app/packages/organizations/tests/integration/bootstrap.test.ts) — 16 cases. Stubs `@monark/feature-flags/server` so the multi-tenant flag is controllable per-test. `getBootstrapStatus` (single-tenant + 0/1/many orgs, multi-tenant always-bootstrapped). `getSingletonOrganization` (null + present). `ensureSingletonOrganizationFromInput` happy path + idempotency (second call returns existing org with `created:false`, original displayName sticks). Guards : multi-tenant short-circuits with `already-multi-tenant`, missing slug or name → `env-not-set`, malformed slug → `invalid-slug` with detail, malformed primaryColor → `invalid-color` with detail, both 3-digit and 6-digit hex accepted. `bootstrapSingletonOrganization` direct : creates + emits, returns existing on a re-call, throws on the >1 org case (operator flipped to single after running multi).

The default `pnpm test` task fails on `web` and `@monark/test-utils` until `pnpm install` picks up the new devDeps ; install-time, resolves once the lockfile commits.

- 2026-05-05: Test plan foundation. Per [docs/technical-documentation/test-plan.md](monark/app/docs/technical-documentation/test-plan.md). New shared [vitest.shared.ts](monark/app/vitest.shared.ts) with v8 coverage provider, lcov + html reporters, and the standard exclude list (re-exports, type-only files, generated code, test fixtures). Every package now has its own `vitest.config.ts` extending the base ; `@vitest/coverage-v8` is wired across the workspace. New `test:coverage` script per package + at the root + as a turbo task ; `pnpm test:coverage` runs the whole monorepo with coverage. Thresholds stay commented-off for the moment — they flip on per package as backlog work closes the gaps. **New test suites** : [`packages/common/tests/{errors,events,result}.test.ts`](monark/app/packages/common/tests/) (28 cases covering every error class, the event-bus single + fan-out + isolation guarantees, and the Result helpers), [`packages/branding/tests/branding.test.ts`](monark/app/packages/branding/tests/) (10 cases : every required field, hex / email / URL validators, template-vars subset purity), and [`packages/notifications/tests/email-shell.test.ts`](monark/app/packages/notifications/tests/email-shell.test.ts) — the regression guard for the 2026-05-05 shell-rendering bug. Iterates every registered `NotificationKind` × `en|fr` (16 combinations) and asserts the rendered HTML / subject / text contain no literal `{{` `}}` substrings, plus that the wordmark + logo URL substitute correctly. Total : 206 tests across the workspace, all green. **CI workflow** : [.github/workflows/ci.yml](monark/app/.github/workflows/ci.yml) split into a fast `verify` job (lint + typecheck + `pnpm test:coverage` + Codecov upload) and a slower `e2e` job that depends on `verify`, boots the Supabase local stack via `supabase start`, runs migrations, installs Chromium + Firefox + WebKit, and runs Playwright. Failure mode uploads the Playwright HTML report as an artefact for 14 days. **Bug found-and-fixed during the rollout** : the existing [`enrich.test.ts`](monark/app/packages/notifications/tests/enrich.test.ts) was asserting against the old `accountLink: ${base}/account` value ; the route-segments work in this same patch had moved it to `/account/danger` (deletion-cancel target) without updating the test. Fixed.

- 2026-05-05: Phase-1 closure pass. Folds in everything that landed since the last CHANGELOG entry without its own line ; phase-2 starts on a clean slate.
  - **Account section moved to real route segments.** `/account?tab=profile|security|notifications|danger` retired in favour of `/account/profile`, `/account/security`, `/account/notifications`, `/account/danger`. New shared layout at [account/layout.tsx](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/account/layout.tsx>) hosts the AppBar + sidebar chrome ; per-tab pages export their section content. New client [account-sidebar.tsx](<monark/app/services/web/src/app/(authed)/account/account-sidebar.tsx>) drives off `usePathname()` instead of `?tab=` query state. Deletion-grace lockdown enforced server-side at the layout (allow-list of `/account`, `/account/profile`, `/account/danger` ; everything else redirects to `/account/danger` while in grace). Notifications inbox moved to its own top-level `/inbox` so the path didn't clash with the preferences tab.
